Group 21 stayed byte-identical in outcome through all five, which is what proves no writer leaked into the capture path.
  - **The retention gap was the crux, and closing it flipped a pin W2101 planted for exactly this moment.** Both cache rewrites kept only `TASK_BASE_REF_<id>`, so every record the engine writes would have died at the next claim. `Get-CarriedWindowRecordLine` re-emits each surviving base record's partners through `Read-TaskRecord` + `ConvertTo-ShSingleQuoted` — never a raw-line copy, never a widened filter, which is the same conclusion D280 reached from the other direction. Test 22p's four "does NOT survive" assertions went red in the same commit that made them wrong and now assert survival **by value**; a count alone would pass a record carried across but mangled in transit. **No orphan** falls out for free: partners derive from the bases that survived, so a partner cannot outlive its base.
  - **Test 22r was updated, not deleted**, as W2101 instructed. It pinned "the record writers have no production call site"; this task added the first, so it is now an **inventory** — a per-writer expected count with the provenance of every call's value recorded. A count of zero could only say nobody calls these; the inventory says who does and where each value comes from, so an unreviewed new call site — in particular one whose value comes off the API response — breaks it.
  - **Two of the four classifier mutants walked straight through the first version of the tests.** Reversing the D256 subset operands survived, because the sibling geometry answers the same either way; a nested-window case now isolates the direction. Defaulting the residual to 0 instead of the full set size survived, because "not the sentinel" still passes when the siblings wrongly subtract — one later commit survives to form a run. The sibling case now asserts the over-report **positively**. That second one mattered most: 0 is the one value that fails toward subtracting a span, i.e. toward losing another author's work.
  - **Two fixture errors were corrected rather than papered over**, and both were mine misreading the algorithm: a four-commit window with no owned record has residual 4 and correctly reads AMBIGUOUS, so expecting a sentinel there was wrong (bash agrees); and the sentinel case needs a window that classifies PURE, so it spans one commit. A third fixture seeded window heads on disk, but the engine reads heads from the **environment** by design — that asymmetry is deliberate, and a disk-seeded fixture would have passed the open-window cases vacuously.
  - **The empty-set guard in the fixpoint is unreachable today, and that is now recorded in the code** rather than left as an untested branch. Phase B skips empty rev-lists, so no pool set can be empty; mutation-checking confirmed removing the guard changes nothing. It is kept because `IsSubsetOf` on an empty set returns TRUE — relax that filter without it and every residual collapses to 0, everything reads PURE, and D244 reopens. The two belong together, and bash carries the same pair.
  - **What is still not ported is listed with a named blocker**: the D268/D274 eviction policy (W2103 — this side still caps kept windows by COUNT, the shape D274 deleted from bash), the D273 self-heal replay (nothing to replay from, since the state file persists no `base=`/`narrowed=`), and one deliberate divergence where the re-emit drops a malformed head/owned value that bash keeps. Test Group 24's banner records every omitted sh Group 23 case with its reason, per acceptance criterion 3.
  - **Round 1 review found seven issues, and three were tests of mine that could not fail.** The two classifier cases asserted that an interior commit was absent from the output — which holds under *every* mutation, because a covered commit is never a run boundary and an uncovered one there is interior to the run. Both now pin the exact range list, which is what makes the classification observable, and the D255 supersession the task's own testing_strategy names is pinned for the first time. Three further branches had no assertion at all: flipping the horizon comparison from `-gt` to `-ge`, deleting the ancestor check, or moving the owned-set cap from `-gt 20` to `-ge 20` each passed the whole suite. All three now fail.
  - **A clock-source split made the D271 gate silently inert on the shipping host.** `Test-AnotherOpenWindowExists` read the clock with `Get-Date -UFormat %s` while both writers of the stamp it compares against use `UtcNow` — and on Windows PowerShell 5.1, the host `stride-hook.sh` actually execs, `-UFormat %s` computes the epoch from **local** time. West of UTC every fresh window yields a negative age and is dropped; east of +4h every fresh window ages out immediately. Either way the predicate answered "no open window" unconditionally and the narrowing never engaged. The reader now uses the writer's expression, and the fixtures build their stamps the way the **writer** does — a fixture sharing the reader's expression is self-consistent by construction and can never see the two disagree, which is exactly why a green suite hid it.
  - **`Test-AnotherOpenWindowExists` had no positive control.** Every one of its negative assertions is satisfied by the predicate returning `False` for *any* reason, so an empty fixture SHA, an unreadable cache and a genuine correct verdict were indistinguishable. It now asserts the fixture is sane and that the same cache shape still reads LIVE.
  - **Two divergences from the reference were closed rather than argued away.** The attributed ranges are now computed **once**, with the base, so the pre-loop and post-loop captures classify identically as bash's do; and the before_review self-heal now attributes too, since bash's equivalent states attribution belongs to every non-refused path — passing `''` there left the one path that can still build a snapshot absorbing its nested children's commits, the exact over-report this task closes everywhere else.
  - ps1 suite: **802 passed, 0 failed** (was 749) — `pwsh -NoProfile -File hooks/test-stride-hook.ps1`. bash suite **787 passed, 0 failed**, with `hooks/stride-hook.sh` and `hooks/test-stride-hook.sh` both byte-unchanged (verified by `git diff --numstat` returning no rows). 5.1 static gate clean.


- **W2101 — the per-task record layer is ported to `stride-hook.ps1`, giving the window/attribution subsystem somewhere to persist a verdict.** Five families — `TASK_BASE_REF`, `TASK_HEAD_REF`, `TASK_OWNED`, `TASK_BASE_AT`, `TASK_NARROWED` — of which only the first existed here, and only as an inline env read; the other four appeared solely as string filters in the env fence, so nothing on this side could read or write them. `Get-TaskRecordKey`, five key builders, `Read-TaskRecord`, per-family readers and four writers now mirror the bash twin, covered by test Group 22.

  - **This layer has NO production call site, deliberately.** Deciding *when* to write a record is the D236/D255/D273 orchestration, which is out of scope. Writing one at a moment bash does not would make the two executors' caches diverge in *content* — strictly worse than the current absence, since the same cache is written by one executor and read by the other in a mixed checkout. Test 22r is an explicit tripwire on that, to be **updated rather than deleted** when the orchestration lands.
  - **Two quoting conventions share one file, and the new writers had to match the other one.** The existing ps1 cache writers emit bare `KEY=value`; bash emits `KEY='value'` via `sq_escape` and then literally `source`s the file. Reusing the local idiom would have been shell injection across the implementation boundary, so `ConvertTo-ShSingleQuoted` reproduces `sq_escape` exactly — 22d proves it by shelling out to bash's own function and comparing outputs for ten probes, and 22f proves the escaped line survives a real `source` round-trip.
  - **TWO byte-level divergences were corrupting the cache on the shipping host, and the first fix only closed one of them.** `Write-EnvCache` used `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8`, which is BOM-less under pwsh 7 but writes a **BOM** under Windows PowerShell 5.1 — the interpreter `stride-hook.sh` execs — making the first line's key unmatchable to bash's `^KEY=`. Switching to `WriteAllLines` fixed that and introduced the second half: it terminates with `Environment.NewLine`, i.e. **CRLF** on that same host, so bash's `^KEY='...'$` still failed on the trailing CR and a *sourced* value kept it — `TASK_BASE_REF` would reach git as `<sha>\r`. Review caught the half-fix. The terminator is now written explicitly as LF, and the byte assertions were moved OUT of the bash-only guard where they had been unrunnable on the only host that can produce either fault.
  - **A test I wrote to catch vacuous checks was itself vacuous.** 22o's BOM assertion sat inside an `if ($g22Bash)` block although reading bytes needs no bash, so it skipped on Windows PowerShell 5.1 — the only host where a BOM can occur — and on pwsh 7 a BOM is impossible by construction, making it true by definition wherever it did run. It is now 22o2, host-independent, with a sibling assertion that no CR precedes any LF.
  - **Filed, not fixed: `Set-HookEnv` writes server-supplied values UNQUOTED into the cache bash sources (D280, critical).** The record layer added here is correctly escaped and is now the *only* correctly escaped writer in the file; the others emit bare `KEY=value`, and a security review confirmed the `. "$ENV_CACHE"` sink executes a `$(...)` payload. Outside this task's hunks, pre-existing, and cross-cutting — but the fix extends the escaper this task ships and has already proven byte-identical to `sq_escape`.
  - **Two bash asymmetries were ported faithfully rather than tidied.** `TASK_BASE_REF` and `TASK_HEAD_REF` are read from the **environment** while the other three go through the file's shape check — the env read *is* weaker, but these two feed the orphan-base guard and the snapshot-base resolution, so making this side stricter would have the two executors disagree about whether a window is usable. And the delete filter on write is broad (`^KEY=`) while the read is strict (`^KEY='...'$`), which is what lets a write clean up a forged line its own reader would never have honoured. Both are recorded as known asymmetries for G413 to revisit on both platforms at once, never on one.
  - **One deliberate divergence, in the safe direction:** a value containing CR, LF or NUL is refused rather than written. Bash would write it faithfully, but the record would then span two physical lines and both readers' full-line check would reject it — and unlike bash, this port's bulk loader has no shape check, so a multi-line record here would plant a forged `KEY=value` straight into the process environment.
  - **The escaping is the security boundary, and the tests prove it rather than assert it.** A server value carrying a newline on an *allowed* key becomes an unquoted second line, invisible to the strict reader; to forge the shape an attacker must supply a `'`, and both escapers turn any `'` into `'\''`, which breaks the `[^']*` class. 22h drives that exact forged-continuation payload. The single-quote limitation of `[^']*` is **pinned** by 22f rather than hidden, because fail-closed is the correct direction.
  - **Group 22 needed a harness the suite did not have.** Dot-sourcing the hook is impossible — its `exit 0` would terminate the *suite* — and a test-local mirror of the escaper would prove nothing about the escaping the hook performs. The functions are extracted from the shipped file by AST and dot-sourced individually, with a completeness check so a missing extraction fails loudly instead of passing vacuously. Verified non-vacuous by deliberately breaking the escaper: 22d and 22f go red, and restoring returns the suite to green.
  - **Criterion 4 cites the wrong line.** The "only the TASK_BASE_REF family … the other four are not ported" comment is at `test-stride-hook.ps1:2799`, not 2697 (which is an unrelated D259 test). The real comment is corrected, test 10n now asserts the collision guard across all five families as the bash twin does, and two further claims this change falsified — the parity note's "has never written one" and the claim-branch note's "none of those families has a reader" — are corrected in the same commit.
  - **An exploratory session drove BOTH implementations against one shared cache — the comparison neither suite makes — and found six divergences.** One was mine and is fixed here: `Read-TaskRecord` used `Get-Content`, which silently strips a trailing CR, so a CRLF-terminated record read `FOUND` here and `ABSENT` to bash. That directly contradicted the no-`Trim` rule three lines above it, which exists precisely so the two executors agree on what a record *is* — a cmdlet doing the stripping is the same act, just not by our hand. It now reads raw and splits on LF only, and 22h2 asserts both sides agree.
  - **The session also confirmed D280 by execution and made it worse.** The command-execution path is real (an inert marker file was created), but the finding that matters needs no attacker: a task title containing an **ordinary apostrophe** leaves an unbalanced quote, so bash's `source` swallows the rest of the file and every variable after it — including the `TASK_BASE_REF_<id>` window records — comes out unset, silently, because the loader's `2>/dev/null || true` eats the diagnostic. A newline forges `TASK_BASE_REF_TRUSTED=1`; `Fix login` made bash execute `/usr/bin/login` and stall a blocking hook to its timeout. D280 was updated with all of it.
  - **Four further divergences share one root cause and are filed as D281.** bash's writer is byte-faithful and its reader byte-oriented; the ps1's writer is a transcoding rewrite and its loader text-oriented — so the ps1 is *more permissive* about what a record is and *less faithful* about what it says. The sharpest instance: a multi-line task description containing a line shaped like `TASK_BASE_REF_42=…` makes the two executors hold **different base-ref SHAs for the same task from the same bytes**, in exactly the ordering `apply_env_lines` already writes. Filed rather than fixed because which side is correct is a design decision the codebase has not made, and four separate patches would drift apart again.
  - **Confirmed parity is recorded too, not just the failures:** valid non-ASCII (Cyrillic, CJK, emoji+ZWJ, combining sequences) round-trips byte-for-byte in both directions; a ps1 rewrite preserves a bash-written cache byte-identically for all valid content including blank lines, comments, tabs and a 5,000-character value; duplicate resolution is last-well-formed-wins identically on both sides; and four interleaved cross-executor writes left every record equal.
  - **The reader/writer split that produced the CRLF divergence is closed at the source, not patched twice.** Fixing `Read-TaskRecord` alone left the *writer* still doing the act the reader now refuses: `Set-TaskRecord` read the surviving lines with `Get-Content`, so any unrelated write re-terminated a CRLF record as LF and **promoted** a line both executors correctly called absent into a first-class record — the forged continuation the claim-branch comment warns about — while a value carrying a bare CR was split in two, unbalancing the quoting for bash's `source`. Both now go through one byte-faithful splitter, `Get-EnvCacheLine`, so a future edit cannot desynchronise them again. The same function fixes the sibling fault named in that comment block: every `ReadAllText` overload sets `detectEncodingFromByteOrderMarks`, which **eats** a UTF-8 BOM, so the first line of a cache written by a pre-fix ps1 under 5.1 read as a clean record here and as absent to bash's `^KEY=`. It reads bytes and decodes explicitly, so the BOM survives into the shape check and both executors agree. This is one instance of the D281 root cause, fixed because the code is new in this change; the rest of D281 stands.
  - **The integration test the strategy named is now real, and it pins a gap rather than blessing one.** "Two sequential claims leave the outer task's records intact" had no counterpart anywhere in the suite, and by inspection it would have failed: the parseable claim branch — the normal path — carries only `TASK_BASE_REF_<id>` across a claim, dropping the other four families, where the bash twin re-emits its window families through `read_task_record` + `sq_escape`. 22p drives two sequential parseable claims and asserts both halves by value: the outer task's base anchor survives with its own value, and the four other families **do not**, labelled as the known gap that G413's D236/D255/D273 orchestration closes. It is written to go red the moment that lands, so the fix cannot ship past a test still asserting the old behaviour. Widening the preservation filter is explicitly *not* the fix — that is what would promote a forged continuation.
  - **Coverage closed against the strategy's own targets:** all four writable families now carry cross-executor assertions in **both** directions (22o4 adds `TASK_NARROWED` and `TASK_BASE_AT`, and drives a cache written by bash's real `write_env_cache` rather than by .NET, so the encoding and terminator half of the bash→ps1 direction is tested too, including a quote-bearing value both sides agree is absent); and the "truncated or partially-written cache" edge case is covered directly (22h3: zero-byte, newline-only, truncated mid-value, truncated before the closing quote, unterminated final line, and the BOM case bash agrees on).
  - **A specialist security pass over the same diff returned two `partial` consideration verdicts, and both trace to code this change did not write.** The new record writers create no route — the fence is untouched, the key builder refuses every non-digits-only server id, values are escaped byte-identically to `sq_escape`, CR/LF/NUL are refused, and the writers have no production call site at all. What is still true at the *file* level is that two OTHER writers — the claim identity block and `Set-HookEnv` — emit server-supplied `TASK_TITLE=` bare into the same sourced cache. That is **D280**, already open, and one newly-identified contribution was recorded onto it: this change's BOM/CRLF fix removed an accidental barrier that had made bash's strict shape check reject a forged CRLF-terminated record on Windows PowerShell 5.1. The BOM/CRLF fix is required for correctness and is not reverted; D280 is the right place to close the hole, and its fix is now known to be a coupled writer-plus-loader change. Three findings landing in this change's own code were fixed: `Get-EnvCacheLine` resolves its path absolutely before `ReadAllBytes` (the same `GetUnresolvedProviderPathFromPSPath` guard `Write-EnvCache` already uses, and it is now the only `[System.IO.File]::*` call in the record layer); `Read-TaskRecord`'s shape check anchors with `\z` rather than `$`, so the reader no longer depends on an LF-free-lines invariant held in another function; and the byte-faithfulness claim is narrowed to valid UTF-8, with 22h5 pinning the invalid-byte seam and folding it into D281. Throwing on invalid bytes was considered and rejected — it would blind the reader to every record in a file containing one bad byte while bash keeps reading the valid lines, a worse divergence than the one it closes.
  - ps1 suite: **674 passed, 0 failed** (was 593) — verified by `pwsh -NoProfile -File hooks/test-stride-hook.ps1`. bash suite **787 passed, 0 failed** (`bash hooks/test-stride-hook.sh`), unchanged with no bash file touched. 5.1 static gate clean across all four `hooks/*.ps1` (`./scripts/check-ps1-compat.sh`).

- **W2100 — `stride-hook.ps1` now BUILDS the changed-files snapshot instead of only uploading one, ending a silent native-Windows gap.** The ps1 hook had zero occurrences of `capture_changed_files`: it PUT a `.stride-changed-files.json` that something else was assumed to have written, and on native Windows that something is nobody — `stride-hook.sh` execs the ps1 and exits. A native-Windows run therefore produced **no snapshot at all**, with no error. This entry retires the "degrade silently to current behavior" note recorded at CHANGELOG.md:1689 when the upload half shipped. Every later child of G413 needs a snapshot to exist before it has anything to narrow or attribute.

  - **What was ported, faithfully.** `Build-ChangedFilesSnapshot` mirrors `capture_changed_files` and `Expand-OwnRanges` mirrors `expand_own_ranges`, including the contract sh Test Group 7 pins: a bare JSON array of `{path, diff}`; the `[diff truncated at 500 lines]` marker appended as the final line after the first 499, so a truncated diff is exactly 500 lines and one of exactly 500 is **not** truncated; the em-dash binary placeholder; and a well-formed `[]` for an empty change set rather than an absent file, which the server distinguishes explicitly. `Resolve-TaskSnapshotBase` and `Resolve-SnapshotBaseTrust` port `select_task_snapshot_base` and `resolve_snapshot_base` — including **both** refusals, which previously had no counterpart here because there was no diff to refuse.
  - **Three PowerShell 5.1 traps the port had to walk around, each invisible on pwsh 7.** `ConvertTo-Json -AsArray` does not exist on 5.1 (added in 6.2), so entries are serialized individually and the array is wrapped by hand. `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` writes a **BOM** on 5.1, and the upload base64s the file's raw bytes verbatim, so a BOM would ride onto the wire and fail the server's decode — the snapshot is written with `UTF8Encoding($false)` and test 21bb pins it. A literal em dash in a BOM-less `.ps1` decodes as the ANSI codepage on 5.1, so the binary placeholder is built from `[char]0x2014` rather than typed.
  - **Two bugs found by the tests, both mine, both the same shape — an argument in the wrong position that fails silently rather than loudly.** `--output` was appended to the git argument list, but everything after a `--` separator is a *pathspec*, so git read it as a filename and every tracked-file diff came back empty. And the new fixture's `.gitignore` patterns were unanchored, so they matched at any depth and would have made the "a same-named file in a SUBDIRECTORY is kept" case pass vacuously by never presenting the file.
  - **`--output` rather than capturing stdout, deliberately.** PowerShell splits native stdout into lines and drops the CR of every CRLF, and re-decodes through `[Console]::OutputEncoding`. Routing git's own `--output` to a temp file outside the project and reading its bytes preserves both. Test 21y proves it — and pins its own line-ending policy in the fixture, because a machine with `core.autocrlf=input` (the case here) strips the CR before a diff exists, which would otherwise have the assertion silently testing the contributor's git config instead of this code.
  - **NOT ported, said out loud rather than implied.** The D236/D255/D273 nested-task narrowing *orchestration* has no ps1 counterpart. The engine is range-capable — `-OwnRanges` and `Expand-OwnRanges` are ported — so adding it later is a call-site change, but every call site passes `''` today. The cost, stated in the corrected PARITY NOTE: on this host an **outer** task's snapshot still contains commits its nested tasks made. That is an over-report, never an under-report, and never another task's diff. The self-heal likewise builds only when no snapshot exists rather than re-capturing, because `Write-DiffUploadState` records no `base=`/`narrowed=` line to replay and re-deriving one at retry time is the exact divergence D273 added persistence to prevent.
  - **A security review caught the port introducing a disclosure surface the twin does not have, and it is fixed.** `Invoke-GitCapture` originally staged raw git output under the system temp directory. git's `--output` creates that file at 0666-minus-umask, so on POSIX hosts — Linux CI, WSL, contributor machines — every file's unified diff sat briefly in a world-readable `/tmp`, including the accidentally-committed secret the capture's bounds exist to contain. Bash has no such window: `mktemp` gives it 0600. Staging moved to `.stride/`, which inherits the project directory's permissions, is already hard-excluded from the capture by the `^\.stride/` rule so it can never surface as an entry, and is where `Write-EnvCache` already stages. Verified: a real capture now leaves the system temp count unchanged and no staging residue behind. Also restored `task_record_key`'s rejection of the reserved `TRUSTED`/`OWNER`/`UNPROVEN` suffixes, which the port had dropped — unreachable behind the digits-only id gate, but bash carries it as insurance for the day that rule widens, and losing it silently in a port is how insurance disappears.
  - **Test Group 21 mirrors sh Group 7 (21a–21w) and adds seven cases it has no equivalent for** (21x–21cc): an end-to-end truncation that binds the pure-logic mirrors to the real implementation, CRLF preservation, a path containing spaces, a renamed binary, the BOM guard, and a foreign-owned base being refused. ps1 suite: **593 passed, 0 failed** (was 530). Two existing cases were repaired rather than left passing on a premise that no longer holds — 7e and 14b both asserted against a pre-seeded snapshot that the hook now overwrites, and their fixtures are real repositories so the same expectations are checked against capture-side exclusion, the stronger enforcement point.
  - **A head-to-head exploratory pass — running both implementations over identical scratch repos, which the bash suite never does — confirmed the `-z` fix mattered more than it looked.** Against the pre-fix build, one Cyrillic directory cost **three of five** entries: the untracked files under it were silently dropped while the hook exited 0 and printed `status: success`. Isolated precisely — `core.quotePath false` restored full parity — and re-verified as fixed against the current build. The same pass also found the drop notice never named the path it dropped, which is now corrected: a dropped entry is the same class of event as a missing snapshot, and an unnamed notice leaves no way to tell which file went.
  - **That pass also recorded broad confirmed parity, which is a result in its own right.** Newlines match byte for byte — all-CRLF, mixed LF/CRLF, lone-CR, and a trailing CR with no final newline — and truncation agrees exactly on CRLF bodies at both the 500 and 501 boundary, including under `core.autocrlf=true` (the Git-for-Windows default). Deletions staged and unstaged, detached HEAD, all three degenerate repos (`[]` on both sides), non-UTF-8 content, and every JSON-escaping case tested were semantically identical, and every snapshot either side produced was valid JSON.
  - **Two Major defects in the BASH reference were found and filed, not fixed here** (the task's first pitfall is that bash is the reference): **D278** — `capture_changed_files` lists paths without `-z`, so any non-ASCII path is written as git's quoted octal spelling *and* gets an empty diff body, because the quoted form matches no pathspec; in one fixture that was 100% of the change set, and it is live on every macOS/Linux agent. **D279** — its line counter is quadratic on long single lines: a 200KB single-line diff took **78s** against the 120s `after_doing` ceiling where this port takes ~1s, and a 400KB diff produced no snapshot at all within 120s. Both are the same invisible-loss class this port was written to end, still live on the platform most agents actually run.
  - **A second security pass found a destructive argument-injection path in the new code, verified against real git and now closed.** The untracked-file diff call lacked a `--` end-of-options separator, so a repository containing a directory named `--output=..` yields the repo-relative path `--output=../victim` — which git parses as an OPTION and opens with `xfopen(arg,"w")` during option parsing, truncating a file **one level above the project root** to 0 bytes before it reaches its usage error. Filenames come from `git ls-files --others`, so the name is chosen by whatever is on disk. Fixed with the separator and, belt-and-braces, by rejecting any dash-leading path in `Test-SafeRepoPath` so a future call site that forgets one still cannot be steered. Reproduced end to end against the fix: the victim file survives intact and the path is refused by name. The bash twin has the identical unguarded shape.
  - **Two independent passes caught the same regression in the `-z` fix, and it is pinned now.** `Out-String` appends a trailing newline after the final NUL, which survived the split as a phantom entry, failed the control-character rule, and printed a drop notice on **every** capture in any repo with an untracked file — making a real omission indistinguishable from noise, which is precisely what naming the path was meant to fix. The terminator is stripped before splitting, and 21q now asserts that a clean capture emits no drop notice at all. The notice also escapes control characters now, so a hostile filename cannot rewrite the terminal or forge log lines while being reported.
  - **Three smaller honesty and latent-bug fixes.** The staging temp is prefixed `capture.` and swept at the start of each run, the system-temp fallback is gone (an unwritable project directory means the snapshot cannot be written either, so falling back only relocated diff content somewhere world-readable), and the guard is `-PathType Container` so a stray `.stride` FILE cannot silently empty every capture. `Expand-OwnRanges` joined chunks with a newline while both call sites request `-z`, which would corrupt the boundary record the moment the orchestration is ported — it now joins with nothing under `-z`. And the claim that 7e2 pins the `-AsArray` fix was **withdrawn**: on pwsh 7 both forms are byte-identical, so 7e2 would pass either way, and the gate cannot see parameters — 7e2 pins the filter, and the 5.1 binding failure stays unpinned until D277 adds a parameter-aware check.
  - **A final security pass found the same option-injection shape one operand over — on the BASE REF — and it was the worst of the set.** A base ref is a *revision*, so `--` cannot protect it: an unguarded `git diff --name-only <base> HEAD` with `TASK_BASE_REF=--output=../victim` does not merely truncate that out-of-tree file, it writes the repo's changed-file **names** into it — an arbitrary-file overwrite whose content is chosen by what exists in the tree. `TASK_BASE_REF` is loaded from `.stride-env-cache` with no key allow-list and no validation, so a repository that ships that file supplies the value, and this change is what made the line reachable on a native-Windows run by removing the early-return guards. Closed at the single producer — `Resolve-TaskSnapshotBase` now rejects an option-shaped base for both halves, so they cannot drift — plus a `rev-parse --verify` gate at the sink so it is safe on its own terms. Pinned by 21dd, which reproduces the attack and asserts the out-of-tree file survives byte-for-byte.
  - **Every value that reaches stderr from the environment or the filesystem now goes through one neutralizer.** The drop notice was escaped when it was added, but three base-ref notices still echoed `.stride-env-cache` values verbatim — so an ESC could rewrite the agent's terminal and a CR/LF could forge additional `stride-hook:` lines, including a forged REFUSING or success line, in the one channel a refused capture has. `ConvertTo-PrintableForLog` renders every byte below 0x20 and 0x7F as `\xNN` and bounds the length, and all four sites use it.
  - **Two stale comments and a sweep race, all found by review rather than by tests.** `Invoke-GitCapture`'s header still claimed the temp lived in the system temp "never inside the project" — the precise property the staging fix had just changed, so a maintainer reading only the header could have reintroduced the world-readable staging — and a leftover sentence still described a fallback that no longer exists. Both corrected. The `capture.*` sweep was also unconditional, which would delete a temp a **concurrent** hook in the same project had open: on POSIX the unlink succeeds while git keeps writing to the unlinked inode, so that run's diff body silently becomes an empty string. It is now age-gated, so cleaning up after the silent-loss class cannot create a new instance of it.
  - **The 5.1 static gate from W2099 passes** — and did not catch the two 7-only *parameters* found in the existing upload path while doing this work, because it checks cmdlet names and not parameters. That blind spot is documented in README's "What this gate cannot see"; the defect itself is filed separately rather than folded in here.

- **W2099 — `hooks/*.ps1` is now gated against Windows PowerShell 5.1, and the gate proves itself on every run.** `stride-hook.sh` execs `powershell.exe` (5.1), but every PowerShell change in this repo has only ever been executed under pwsh 7, so a 7-only construct passes every local run and fails only on a user's Windows box. `scripts/check-ps1-compat.sh` runs PSScriptAnalyzer's `PSUseCompatibleSyntax` (`TargetVersions = 5.1`) and `PSUseCompatibleCmdlets` (`desktop-5.1.14393.206-windows`) over `hooks/*.ps1`, wired into the bash suite as **Test Group 29**. It lands before the ~2,000 lines of new PowerShell G413 will add, not after.

  - **Zero findings on the current files, therefore zero suppressions — and that vacuity is stated rather than hidden.** All four `hooks/*.ps1` files are already clean against both rules. No `.ps1` source was modified and no `SuppressMessageAttribute` was added anywhere: a suppression for a finding the analyzer never emits would be a false claim of coverage. The value here is forward-looking.
  - **The settings file's `Enable` key is asymmetric, and both failure modes are silent.** `PSUseCompatibleSyntax` **requires** `Enable = $true` or it never fires; `PSUseCompatibleCmdlets` **must not** be given `Enable` or it is dropped. Each was reproduced by running one 7-only probe through all three shapes. A symmetric-looking settings file — the natural result of "tidying" it — reads as correct, passes review, and certifies nothing.
  - **So the gate re-proves both rules against an in-memory probe before every scan**, asserting **per rule** rather than on a total, since a total-only check passes in exactly the half-misconfiguration above (and parse errors arrive as findings, padding totals). It uses `-ScriptDefinition`, so no scratch file is written and no cleanup can fail. Group 29's second case independently points the gate at a deliberately 7-only file and requires it to go red naming both rules: a compatibility gate that cannot fail is worse than no gate, because it certifies.
  - **Scope is all four `hooks/*.ps1`, test suites included, matching the criterion's literal glob.** `hooks/test-stride-hook.ps1` is the artifact a Windows contributor runs to prove the hook works, so its 5.1-parseability is a real property; and an exclusion list would be a silent coverage hole the next `hooks/*.ps1` would have to be remembered into. A scan matching zero files exits non-zero — a vacuous scan is not a pass.
  - **PSScriptAnalyzer is a documented prerequisite, never vendored and never auto-installed.** The install command pins `-RequiredVersion 1.25.0` so a fresh install cannot silently take a different build, while the runtime check uses `-MinimumVersion` so a contributor already on a newer build is not blocked, and the resolved version prints on every run. Nothing in the hook path invokes the gate: installing modules on a user's machine is not an acceptable hook side effect. Absent tooling exits `2` and **skips** in the suite — as the suite already does for `jq`, `git` and `python3` — rather than turning it permanently red on machines without pwsh.
  - **Three silent-pass shapes were found in review and closed, all of the same class: a green result that had stopped meaning anything.** (a) The runtime accepts any build at or above the pin so a contributor on a newer analyzer is not blocked, and the resolved version was printed — but Group 29 captured the gate's output and echoed it only on skip or failure, so on the pass path that ran routinely, version drift was invisible. Drift now emits a `warn:` line that the pass branch surfaces, and `STRIDE_PS1_GATE_REQUIRED=1` converts an absent-tooling skip into a failure for CI, where a green suite that gated nothing is worst. (b) Group 29's negative case asserted that the failing output "names both rules", which the self-test's own success banner satisfies unconditionally — leaving the exit code as the only real check, and reporting a *passing* negative test in exactly the broken state it exists to detect. It now requires an `INCOMPATIBLE:` finding per rule and the absence of a self-test failure. (c) The scan does not recurse, which matches the documented `hooks/*.ps1` scope but would silently ignore a future `hooks/<subdir>/*.ps1`; nested files are now named in a `warn:` rather than passed over. A second positional path, or an empty one, is likewise now an error instead of quietly changing what was scanned.
  - **An exploratory session against the gate found three more ways it could report clean over incompatible code; all are closed.** (a) The `SuppressMessageAttribute` the gate's own failure message recommended, placed above a file-level `param()`, silences a rule for the **entire file** — a file with seven distinct 7-only constructs came back `clean`, exit 0, with the self-test still printing its reassuring line, because the self-test runs against its own in-memory probe and cannot see a suppression. The message now says to attach it to the enclosing *function* and explains why, and the gate warns whenever a compatibility suppression is present, so a blinded file no longer looks identical to a clean one. (b) Directory enumeration dropped subdirectory `.ps1` and sibling `.psm1` files silently, so one clean top-level file carried an unscanned tree to green; skipped files are now named. (c) `--self-test PATH` discarded the path and scanned nothing while exiting 0 — the most plausible wrong invocation being the most reassuring-looking one — and is now an error. Also: a file whose bytes do not decode as script text (BOM-less UTF-16, binary) reported `clean` because the analyzer returns zero findings for what it cannot read; that is now a gate error, detected on embedded NULs rather than emptiness, since BOM-less UTF-16 decodes to plausible-looking text. And parse errors and `FileReadError` were printed under the `INCOMPATIBLE:` label with advice to rewrite a construct — which walked a reader debugging a typo straight into the suppression trap; non-compatibility diagnostics are now labelled `UNANALYZABLE:` and the rewrite guidance is suppressed for them.
  - **The last vacuous-clean door: `Invoke-ScriptAnalyzer` has no `-LiteralPath`, and `-Path` globs.** A filename containing `[`, `]`, `*` or `?` resolved to nothing, returned zero findings, and printed `ok: <file> - clean` for a file that was never analyzed — verified with a `we[ir]d.ps1` holding both a ternary and `Get-Uptime`. The path is now wildcard-escaped, and that file is correctly flagged. Every other path operation in the script already used `-LiteralPath`; this was the one call that still globbed. Two reviews disagreed about it — one asserted `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'` made it fail closed and red — so it was settled by execution rather than by argument.
  - **What held up under that session is worth recording too:** nesting never hid a construct the rules know (a ternary inside string interpolation, a here-string, and a scriptblock parameter default were all still flagged); BOMs, CRLF, latin-1 and missing trailing newlines hid nothing; every file-access and parse failure failed safe with exit 1 and never a raw error record; there were zero false positives across five 5.1-legal controls; the per-rule self-test fired on all 11 probe invocations; and a 6 MB / 250,000-line file was analyzed at the correct line in about two seconds.
  - **This is a syntax and cmdlet-name gate only, and its blind spots are documented rather than implied.** README's new "What this gate cannot see" lists each, confirmed by execution: 7-only *parameters* on cmdlets that exist in 5.1 (`-Parallel`, `-AsByteStream`, `-AsHashtable`) are invisible; so is the .NET surface, including the 3-argument `[System.IO.File]::Move` behind the `Move-Item -Force` delete-then-move divergence already written up at `hooks/stride-hook.ps1:40-46`; so is `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest`, which is valid on both hosts but resolves differently; so is `$PSStyle`; and the cmdlet profile itself has data gaps (`Join-String` is 7-only and goes unflagged). Runtime verification on a real Windows host remains a separate, unfinished job.

### Changed

- **W2098 — the Session Position share branch gets a checkable threshold, and the derivation turns up two figures it must not inherit.** The section previously stated its own gap ("no checkable threshold for that judgement exists yet… the policy deliberately stays a judgement call") because nothing priced the *value* of reuse the way the isolation-floor derivation prices accumulation. It now carries a break-even in the isolation floor's own shape — `rediscovery_tokens > carried_tokens × requests_per_task × (N − k)` — with each input sourced, and it remains a **heuristic with stated assumptions, never an absolute rule**, because one input is genuinely unmeasured.

  - **The inherited-assumption check (an acceptance criterion) found the borrowed figure is wrong for this branch by ~12×.** The isolation floor's **4 main-loop requests per task** comes from the cap derivation in `docs/task-runner-contract.md:415-418`, where it describes a **dispatcher-mode runner** and is explicitly flagged unmeasured at `:786-788`. Its use *there* is faithful — `reference.md:339` already says "neither of which is measured", so no correction was needed. But this section governs **human-operator sessions working inline**, and those are measured: **44, 53 and 59** requests for the three tasks of the measured session (`token-measurement-g404-g405.md:141-145`). The new derivation states the distinction explicitly rather than reusing the 4.
  - **The cost side is derived, not measured, and is labelled so.** Average context per request grew **137,165 → 255,886 → 369,173**, so a completed task adds ~113,000–119,000 tokens resident (deltas 118,721 and 113,287, mean 116,004). Held loosely on two grounds the section states: a delta of per-task *averages* conflates what task `k` left with what task `k+1` grew, and the ordering runs the wrong way — the **small** task's delta exceeds the **medium** one's, which a clean residue should not. `requests_per_task` is likewise flattened from the measured 44/53/59 to ~50, and the source attributes that growth to task size as well as position, so the flattening is flagged as an assumption. The reuse side has no figure anywhere in this repo — `orchestrator-context-isolation-design.md:495-498` says outright "Nobody has measured the split" — and the **29.5% at position 2** figure was considered and **rejected as a substitute**: it prices a task-runner's cold start, not an operator's cleared session, which would be the same silent inheritance the `4` finding caught.
  - **Price, not token count, and the denominator convention is named rather than assumed.** Raw counts make the carry look ~60× dearer, but the sides are different token kinds; on the doc's assumed multipliers (cache write 1.25×, cache read 0.1×) the carry is 580,020 base-equivalents. Against a dispatch's **spin-up base** (92,000 × 1.25 = 115,000) that is **5.04×**; against a **full measured explorer dispatch** it is **2.73×** — that dispatch being W2058's 94,859 write × 1.25 + 938,378 read × 0.1 + 48 output × 5 ≈ **212,652 base-equivalents** (a price-adjusted figure; the same three counts sum raw to 1,033,285). The section uses the full-dispatch figure, because this branch asks "would I rather re-run an explorer than carry this" rather than "does spinning one up repay itself" — the earlier draft silently used the base for a sentence whose named mechanism was a full dispatch. With the doc's session-level counterfactual (28.4M ÷ 2 boundaries) at **6.69×**, the stated bracket is **~3–7×, not 60×**.
  - **No single-figure headline, because the bar moves with position.** The bar is `carried × 50 × (N − k)` with `carried` growing in `k`, so for a five-task goal it runs **11 / 16 / 16 / 11** full-dispatch equivalents across the four boundaries — smallest at the ends, largest in the middle. An unqualified "about five" is the cheapest boundary in the session and under-prices sharing several-fold; the parent paragraph's own reporting rule (a percentage of this class is well-formed only with its position attached) is now honoured by the subsection too.
  - **The structural limit, stated as the honest headline rather than buried.** Where reuse is written down, re-discovery collapses to "read the artifact" — far under any bar, so **clear**; where it is not, `rediscovery_tokens` is precisely the unestimable quantity, so the inequality **returns nothing**. The costed test therefore yields **clear or undefined and does not return share on its own**. That is reported as a finding about what the arithmetic can decide, explicitly **not** as a rule that sharing is wrong: when sharing is right it is right for reasons this costing does not capture (operator re-briefing time, the risk of losing an undocumented decision), which is why the policy stays a judgement call. Noted too that the workflow's own both-channels and report-to-file rules keep moving cases into the written-down half.
  - **Queue order is named as the operator's real lever**, since `k` presupposes an order the board supplies but the operator controls; reorder first, then apply the test. And the model is **explicitly scoped to linear queues** — a task depending on a *non-adjacent* predecessor (the diamond a partially-dependent goal generically produces) has no term in the inequality and is left unpriced rather than silently mis-priced.
  - **Caveats carried per the pitfall.** The ~54% stays an upper bound and confounded, so the 5–6 million is an order of magnitude and the 3–7× a shape rather than a value; the multipliers are assumed, not invoiced; the break-even is position-dependent, which is why the bar is quoted per boundary and never as one number; and where `rediscovery_tokens` cannot be estimated the heuristic **degrades to the judgement call it replaced** rather than fabricating a figure — which, given the structural limit above, is where the whole share branch lives.
  - **The manual test was performed, and its result is recorded including where it failed.** An exploratory session (8 probes, `charter_quiet`, so coverage of the walked queues is complete rather than budget-truncated) walked two constructed queues — **the original W2091 queues are not in this repository and were not re-walked**, which the section's own note and this entry both state. A five-child **building** goal resolved: clear at all four boundaries, two readers agreeing — but the answer is the *opposite* of the policy shorthand for that exact shape, and the chain-length bound never fired because no chain formed. A five-child **partially-dependent** goal resolved only **two of four** boundaries: one where the qualitative share trigger met a bar needing position-correction, one diamond. **So the `testing_strategy` coverage target — one defensible grouping for two operators on both walkthrough queues — is met for the building goal and NOT met for the partially-dependent one.** That is recorded in the section as a stated limit rather than papered over; the session's ten findings were all in lines this task wrote and were all fixed here rather than filed.

- **The isolation-floor derivation's per-report figure was 7× stale, found while sourcing the above.** `reference.md:329` priced subagent reports at **6,261 tokens each** — the pre-G407 baseline. G407/W2071 measured report-to-file cutting what reaches context to **2,646 B ≈ 925 tokens per report**, an 85.2% cut, and `token-measurement-g404-g405.md:676-677` names the per-report into-context figure as the comparable one while warning that session totals are not. Corrected, with the consequence stated as a **position** rather than a single ratio, because that is what actually moved: a medium task's three reports now accumulate ~2,775 tokens, which clears the floor only while `(N − k) > ~8`, so a medium task is worth isolating in roughly the **front half** of a 20-task session and not the back half. Under the superseded 6,261 average the break-even sat at `(N − k) ≈ 1.2` — essentially always — so the correction changes that gate from "always justified for a medium task" to "justified early". Two confounders are now carried with the 2,646 figure rather than left implicit: it was measured over **six dispatches none of which was a planner** (and the planner report is the largest single artefact measured in this repo, 34.8 KB), so applying it to a planner-inclusive task is an extrapolation — defensible because report-to-file bounds every agent's *returned* summary however large its on-disk report grows, but an extrapolation nonetheless. Left in `reference.md`; `SKILL.md` untouched, and the budgeted files are unchanged at 91,089 / 58,792 / 30,376 (`scripts/check-skill-budgets.sh`, exit 0).

### Fixed

- **D264 — the reference layer's copy-pasteable exemplar is swept against every hard rule added to `stride-completing-tasks/SKILL.md` since D251, and every drifted site the sweep and its own exploratory session found is corrected.** W2089 caught the universal-claims instance in the worked example's **JSON body** and fixed it there, but the same string in the **jq invocation four lines up** — the literal an agent actually copy-pastes into their own command — was left uncited. `reference.md:199`'s `--arg notes 'All tests passing. PR #123 created.'` now cites the command that verified it, byte-identical to the JSON body at `reference.md:244` — both landed first as `(after_doing mix test: 230 tests, 0 failures)` and were then revised to the `<N>`/`<M>` placeholder form recorded further down, which is what both lines read today. That divergence was a defect twice over: it modelled the violation the Red Flags entry at `reference.md:389` warns against, and it contradicted the worked example's own prose (`reference.md:228-232`), which licenses the JSON body to be a *fuller* illustration than the jq placeholders but names `completion_notes` as one of the six fields the `--arg` substitutions populate — so those six should agree, and these two did not. W2089's own changelog entry claiming "reference.md's canonical payload cites its verifying command" was therefore true of only one of the payload's two halves; this entry supersedes that claim. Baseline for the sweep: **`84a3eda`** (D251), located by searching the full subrepo log rather than `git log -- SKILL.md`, which does not surface it because D251's own diff touched only `reference.md`. Rule set enumerated by `git log --oneline 84a3eda..HEAD -- skills/stride-completing-tasks/SKILL.md` — **exactly two commits**, both verdicted below. Per-rule verdicts, so the next sweep has a baseline rather than re-deriving one:

  - **Rule A — W2096 (`88e3574`, SKILL.md:271), the older-server conditioning convention for every `response_view`/`fields` mention. Verdict: CLEAN, already reconciled — no edit.** The rule's own commit updated `reference.md` in the same pass (5 insertions). `grep -n 'response_view\|fields=\|slim' reference.md` returns 3 hits and each is conditioned: the QRC endpoint line carries the degrades-safely parenthetical (`reference.md:115-116`), and the worked example's curl is conditioned by the sentence immediately above it (`reference.md:189-191`). Classification: **prose-level, not payload-expressible** — it governs the conditioning clause that must accompany a `response_view`/`fields` mention, so it has applicable sites in `reference.md` (the three above, all conditioned) but none inside an example payload *body*, which is why the verdict is CLEAN at those three sites rather than not-applicable.
  - **Rule B — W2089 (`be2057d`, SKILL.md:502), universal claims in `completion_summary`/`completion_notes` must name the command that verified them. Verdict: DRIFTED at one site, corrected — `reference.md:199`.** Classification: **payload-expressible, hard rule.** In-scope sites enumerated by `grep -n 'completion_notes\|completion_summary\|--arg notes\|--arg summary' reference.md` (**12 hits**: 120, 121, 199, 200, 207, 208, 230, 231, 232, 244, 245, 389): lines 120-121 are bare `"..."` placeholders; lines 200/245 (`'Brief one-line summary for tracking.'`) assert no totality; line 244 was already compliant; lines 207-208/230-232 are field-mapping prose, not claims; line 389 is the Red Flags entry that *models* the rule. That accounts for eleven; the twelfth, line 199, was the sole violation.

  Exemplar surfaces walked against both rules, with the verdict each returned: **worked payload** (jq invocation `reference.md:193-226` — DRIFTED, fixed; JSON body `240-292` — clean); **Quick Reference Card** (`84-183` — clean; conditioned endpoint, placeholder-only completion fields); **Common Mistakes** gallery (`296-373` — not applicable, Mistakes 1-6 carry no `completion_notes`/`completion_summary` content and no `response_view` mention); **Red Flags** (`377-391` — clean, carries the Rule B entry added by W2089); **Rationalization Table** (`393-402` — clean, carries the uncited-universal row added by W2089); **Real-World Impact** (`406-418` — not applicable, aggregate statistics, not a payload exemplar); **flowchart** (`5-64` — not applicable). Two deliberate non-edits **under Rule B**, recorded so a later sweep does not re-open them as findings (both sites were nonetheless changed later on other grounds — see the session bullets below): the `review_report` values at `reference.md:203` and `:248` ("Approved — 0 issues found.") contain a quantifier but sit **outside** Rule B's stated scope, which binds `completion_summary` and `completion_notes` only — reconciling toward SKILL.md means honouring that scope, not widening it; and per the task's own edge-case guidance, Rule A is recorded CLEAN at its three prose sites rather than given an invented *payload* exemplar it cannot have. `SKILL.md` was read-only for the whole rule sweep — every correction above is confined to `reference.md`. It was edited at exactly one point, later and for a different reason: the two timeout digits recorded below, under the key_files carve-out. Its authoritative-where-they-disagree line at `:481` is untouched either way (`git diff` shows changed hunks at `:219` and `:236` only). Byte budgets green: `scripts/check-skill-budgets.sh` budgets the three SKILL.md files only (`reference.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` are unbudgeted), and `stride-completing-tasks/SKILL.md` stands at **58,792 of 64,000** — one byte above its pre-task 58,791, from `60` becoming `600`.

  The task's own exploratory session (12 probes, `stop_reason: probe_budget_exhausted`, so coverage of this exemplar is **partial, not exhaustive**) then walked the corrected example as a hurried first-time agent and found three further defects, all fixed here under the relatedness gate rather than filed. Its decisive probe was **executing** the exemplar's `jq` rather than reading it — which is how it caught what a source-level comparison structurally cannot:

  - **The two halves still diverged at `review_report`, invisibly.** the quoted value strings on `reference.md:203` and `:248` are byte-identical (`od -c` confirmed — the surrounding lines differ, one being a jq `--arg report` flag and the other a JSON `"review_report":` key), but inside a **single-quoted** shell `--arg` value `\n` is not an escape — jq received two literal characters and re-escaped them, so the command emitted `"## Review Summary\\n\\nApproved — 0 issues found."` while the JSON body at `:248` denotes real newlines. An agent copying the `jq` half posted a review report whose heading and paragraph break were literal `\n` text. Line 203 now uses ANSI-C quoting (`$'…'`); the six `--arg`-populated fields were then re-verified **by executing the corrected command** and diffing its output against `reference.md:242-248` — all six agree. This is the **third** recurrence of one class at one site (`84a3eda` is titled "reconcile the worked example with its jq invocation"; W2089 fixed the JSON half of `completion_notes`; this task fixed the `jq` half), because each pass was scoped to the rule it was about rather than to the six-fields-agree property the prose states. Treat that property as a checkable invariant, not something to re-derive.
  - **The corrected citation modelled compliance as a fabricated literal.** `(after_doing mix test: 230 tests, 0 failures)` is evidence-shaped and survives copy-paste untouched, so a hurried agent submitted a specific, false, verified-looking count that cleared **both** compliance surfaces — `SKILL.md:502` and the Red Flags entry at `:389`. That inverts the rule's stated purpose: a citation nobody can trust is spot-checked *less* than a bare claim, so the pre-fix string was hollow-looking while the post-fix one was plausible and equally unverified. Both halves now read `<N> tests, <M> failures`, matching the file's existing angle-bracket placeholder convention (`<verbatim>`, `<file:line>`, `<40+ non-whitespace chars>`): a placeholder cannot be submitted silently, so substitution is forced rather than merely hoped for.
  - **`Mistake 1`'s ✅ block contradicted `SKILL.md` on hook timeouts** — `timeout 120` / `timeout 60` against an authoritative 600s stated at `SKILL.md:182`/`:187` and at five of `reference.md`'s own sites (27, 40, 55, 107, 109), i.e. the file disagreed with itself 5 sites to 2, in the block whose whole function is "copy this one". **The mirror was not the only copy.** `SKILL.md:219` and `:236` are the originals `reference.md` mirrored, and they carried the same stale 120/60 — so `SKILL.md` contradicted *itself*, its prose at `:182`/`:187` against its own copy-pasteable blocks. Fixing only `reference.md` would have made the two files' code blocks disagree where they previously agreed and left the live path in the **higher-authority** file, so all four sites are now 600 (`grep -n 'timeout ' SKILL.md reference.md` → 4 hits, all 600). **This is the one edit that touched `SKILL.md`**, taken under the key_files carve-out ("read-only *unless a rule is itself ambiguous*") on the grounds that a prose-versus-example self-contradiction is exactly that ambiguity; it changes two digits, adds **1 byte** (58,791 → 58,792 of 64,000), introduces no new rule text, and leaves the authoritative-where-they-disagree line at `:481` untouched. Flagged plainly because the task scoped `SKILL.md` read-only: if the intent was that no SKILL.md byte changes under any circumstance, revert those two digits and the reference.md sites will need reverting with them to keep the pair consistent. Reasoned but **not observed**, and recorded as such: the block is the manual-hook path, so a non-Claude-Code agent wrapping `mix test` in `timeout 120` gets a false failure on any suite over 120s, which `Mistake 6` (`reference.md:365`, five entries below `Mistake 1` at `:298`) then directs into a fix-and-retry loop against tests that were never broken.

  Two findings are recorded rather than acted on. **The escape hatch is not a rival exemplar** — the sweep enumerated every in-scope exemplar value by the 12-hit grep above and found none licensing an uncited universal — **it is the Quick Reference Card's earlier, plainer block labelled `REQUIRED BODY` (`reference.md:117-166`), whose `completion_notes` is a bare `"..."` and which carries no rule signal at all**; a cold reader meets both copy surfaces before the rule's first mention at `:389`, and the rule appears in neither of `SKILL.md`'s pre-submission gates, which meanwhile direct freehand authorship into the two bound fields five times — `grep -n 'completion_notes\|completion_summary' SKILL.md` returns 10 hits, of which lines 143, 158, 159, 160 and 335 are authoring directives. That is left alone deliberately, and **not** on the editability grounds the timeout fix argued around: its fix would mean adding new rule-signal text to `SKILL.md`'s gates, which is the "do not grow `SKILL.md`" pitfall in its plain sense — real growth, and a rule-placement redesign rather than the resolution of a self-contradiction. The carve-out that licensed two digits does not stretch to that. And the session's largest **unexamined** risk is cross-repo — whether the five port repositories carry this same exemplar and therefore the same three defects; this sweep was scoped to one repo and did not look.

## [1.67.0] - 2026-08-15

### Changed

- **W2082 — the task-decomposer body drops 46%, and its duplicated request envelope is deleted rather than moved.** 25,521 to 13,684 bytes. The 12.2 KB worked decomposition moves to `docs/task-decomposer-reference.md`; the two JSON envelope examples that restated what `stride-creating-goals` owns are **deleted in favour of a pointer** to that skill, because moving them would have preserved exactly the drift the W2050 de-duplication precedent exists to prevent. The one-line goals-vs-tasks root-key warning stays, reframed as a warning that defers to the skill if the two ever disagree, so there is one authority and one reminder rather than two authorities. The six methodology steps, both sizing tables, the dependency-graph shapes and the per-task field formats stay inline. Verified by dispatching the slimmed body against a large-goal fixture: it returned a batch-valid decomposition and reported the provenance of each envelope fact (root key from the body's warning and the skill, `agent_name` placement from the skill, index dependencies from the body), confirming the pointer chain reaches the owning skill as the de-duplication depends on. Three fixes came out of that run, the notable one being the task's own security consideration: it asked that the treat-task-text-as-data instruction stay inline, and the instruction turned out **never to have existed** in this agent — not in the body and not in the material that moved. The decomposer consumes free-form human goal text and greps arbitrary repository content, so Step 1 now carries the boundary explicitly.

- **W2081 — the task-enricher body drops 23%.** 37,338 to 28,865 bytes. The five edge-case sections, the five common-mistake anti-examples and the fully-populated worked example move to `docs/task-enricher-reference.md`; the four phases and Phase 2's six ordered steps, the field-type contract, the seven behaviour-matrix categories, the never-modify-human-authored-fields rule and the no-secrets rules all stay inline. Placed in `docs/` rather than `agents/` because every `.md` under `agents/` across all five installed Stride plugins carries agent frontmatter and registers as dispatchable — a reference file there would have registered as a broken agent. Verified by dispatching the slimmed body against a title-only task with the real codebase available: it enriched every field without ever opening the reference file. That run found six defects, four introduced by the split — a dangling `Example for a "work" task:` lead-in and `exactly as shown below` with nothing below, a dangling `(as above)`, pointer boilerplate pasted where nothing was enumerated, and the waived-matrix-row shape (`status: "not_applicable"` with no `type` key) being stated **only** by a sentence narrating the removed example, so moving the example silently deleted a rule. Two were pre-existing: `Never invent a path` for `test_name` had no carve-out for the test file of a source file the task itself creates (which measurably distorted the verification run's own matrix), and three places assert `description` is preserved while none said what to do when the human supplied none. All six fixed. The no-secrets rule was field-scoped in three places, leaving `pitfalls`, `patterns_to_follow` and `key_files` notes uncovered while Phase 2 Step 5 actively directs the agent to read credential-handling code; it now has a blanket form covering every field.

- **W2080 — the task-reviewer body drops 15%, and the returned summary gets a rendering specification for the first time.** 66,177 to 55,936 bytes, re-paid on every review round including re-reviews. The two worked examples (the fully-populated `changes_requested` block and its matching returned summary, 12.7 KB) move to `docs/task-reviewer-examples.md`; the block schema, the verdict rules, the bounded-summary contract, the input contract, the prompt-injection framing and the redaction rules all stay inline, and steps 1-8 keep their numbers, so every one of the nine citing files still resolves (verified by grep). Verified the W2053 way rather than by reading the diff — dispatched against the slimmed body with a staged pitfall-violating diff — and the first run **found a real break**: the returned summary's line rendering existed only in the moved example, so two reviewers would have rendered the same review differently on a carrier the orchestrator parses positionally. That rendering is now specified inline (canonical shapes, both conditional renderings, row ordering); before this task it was only ever illustrated. A second run, forbidden from opening the sibling file, produced a canonically-shaped 19-line / 1,206-character summary with every per-line cap holding. Three pre-existing bugs fixed on the way: the `top issues` header cap was 20 characters when the canonical header needs 22 once either count reaches two digits (raised to 24, overflow line dropped 60 to 56 so the by-construction sum stays exactly 2,000); the worked example's summary contradicted its own JSON block on `testing_strategy`, the matrix row tally and three issue paths; and the `Approved` first line had two spellings across the file, one of which orchestrator fallback paths grep for.

- **W2091 — the session-position policy is written down where an operator will find it.** New `## Session Position (Operator Guidance)` section in stride-workflow's reference.md (deliberately out of the byte-budgeted hot path — verified by `bash scripts/check-skill-budgets.sh`, which does not budget reference.md and stays green on the three files it does): unrelated tasks — typically standalone defects — each get a fresh or cleared session; tasks that genuinely build on each other — children of one goal working toward one change — share one, with the two edge cases that cut against the parent/standalone shorthand (independent children of one goal → fresh; a follow-up defect to minutes-old work → share). The measured basis is cited with the measurement doc's own caveats attached: ~54% of the measured three-task session's cost sat in session position against G404's 6.2% — an upper bound, not a measurement (task-size confound), traded against cross-task context that has real value exactly when tasks build on each other, and reported with position-in-session per the doc's own rule (the same change measured 15.2% and 5.6% in one sitting). The measurement doc's recommendation 1 now points at the section; SKILL.md is untouched (verified by `git -C stride diff --stat`, which names only reference.md, the measurement doc, and this changelog). The task's own exploratory session (an operator walking four queue shapes) then hardened the section before completion: the citation spot-check passed in full (every figure verbatim, "nine times" a fair 8.77 rounding), the clean queue shapes each yield one grouping two operators would agree on — and the two gaps it found are now explicit rather than silent: a third edge case says "share" can only extend a session that still exists (a `needs_review=true` child ends the session at Step 8's stop, and an overnight gap usually means the session is gone — the resume-or-clear decision afterwards re-applies the principle to what remains), and the share branch now states plainly that the ~54% upper bound is the measured price of sharing, that no checkable threshold for the reuse-vs-price judgement exists yet, and that the policy deliberately stays a judgement call (per the task's own pitfall) — with the threshold derivation filed as a follow-up rather than invented here.

- **W2089 — universal claims in completion summaries must name their verification.** New hard rule in `stride-completing-tasks`, beside the completion-field reference: any universal quantifier in `completion_summary` or `completion_notes` — *all*, *every*, *never*, *none*, *zero*, *no other*, *nothing*, "complete coverage" — must name the command that verified it (a grep, a test run, a count), placed beside the claim, the way D221's changelog records its verifying grep; a universal no command can verify is rewritten as a bounded claim instead, and ordinary statements stay citation-free (the rule is deliberately not a server-side gate — the D239 lesson that regex-gating free text across runtimes is either toothless or hostile). Motivation: the review-round tax is the biggest unpriced cost in the done-column record — D220, D226, and D227 each took six review rounds, with unverified universals the recurring cause in the agents' own summaries (D221 claimed complete coverage three times before it was true), and each avoided round saves a full reviewer dispatch plus a fix cycle. One paragraph with one worked example; the G409 byte budget stays green (completing SKILL.md 58,791 of 64,000, `scripts/check-skill-budgets.sh`). The task's own exploratory session then hardened the rule before commit, walking it as a hurried agent hunting escape hatches: the binding category is now "claims of totality however worded" — quantifiers (*always* and *only* added to the list) and totality adjectives (*complete*, *comprehensive*, *fully*, *exhaustive*) alike — closing the one-word synonym swap the first draft's "quantifiers only" scope sentence would have licensed; the named command must be one a reviewer could re-run to **falsify** the claim (kind-naming like "checked with a grep", or a command establishing something adjacent, does not satisfy it); the bounded form is stated to be the review-favorable one, so honesty is not priced as an invitation to a round; and the reference layer now models compliance instead of the violation — reference.md's canonical payload cites its verifying command, and the Red Flags list and Rationalization Table each carry the uncited-universal entry (the D251 drift class, caught before shipping this time). Residual accepted as inherent to a prose rule, per the D239 no-regex-gate lesson: quantifier deletion to bare plurals ("tests pass") escapes any word list — a reviewer can still ask "all of them?", and that question is one round cheaper than six.

- **W2097 — the older-server conditioning convention is finished: an unsure-of-the-server-era branch at every index/tree rule site, the last straggler normalized, and every degraded-behaviour claim verified against a real pre-G405 checkout.** All four index/tree rule sites (stride-claiming-tasks SKILL.md, stride-workflow reference.md / platform-other.md / optional-dispatcher-mode.md) now carry the branch the W2096 cold-read found missing: when the reader cannot tell what era their server is, no version header or onboarding field will say (both eras' onboarding `version`/`skills_version` are the same static literals — verified side by side), but one probe half-answers — `fields=column_id` 422s only on a current server, so a 422 proves slim is safe, while a 200 leaves the era unresolved because pre-projection servers span both index/tree eras — and on a 200, or to skip the probe, the reader takes the branch safe on both eras: avoid index/tree and use `next`/show, which degrade safely (the same resolve-by-era-agnostic-pattern shape diff-capture.md's if-you-are-unsure branch already modeled; the one-directional probe was found by this task's own exploratory session, which demonstrated the 422-vs-200 divergence on both eras). diff-capture.md's slim mention — the one pre-convention straggler — now uses the degrades-safely vocabulary; platform-other.md's older-server caveat moves adjacent to the Never rule, matching the other copies; task-runner-contract.md's parenthetical gains the unknowable-era arm. The sweep (`grep -rn --no-ignore-files -e 'response_view' -e 'fields=' stride/skills stride/docs`) found every remaining hit conditioned, a curl example, or an annotated historical record (token-measurement and the context-isolation design doc both carry superseded/historical notes). And the asserted behaviours are no longer merely plausible: against a real pre-G405 checkout (kanban bfc0aa95, scratch database) index, tree, and show served byte-identical bodies with and without `response_view=slim`, `fields=` was silently ignored — `fields=column_id` included, returning the full body with no 422 — and `/complete?response_view=slim` echoed the full 62-key task; the current server 422s `fields=column_id` with the allow-list message and serves the 75-byte projection for allow-listed names. No documented clause was falsified. Hot-path byte budgets stay green (claiming SKILL.md 30,376 of 33,500).

- **W2096 — one consistent older-server conditioning convention for every `response_view` / `fields` mention.** Degrade-UNSAFE sites — the index/tree slim rule at its four sites (stride-workflow `reference.md`, `platform-other.md`, `optional-dispatcher-mode.md`, and the previously unlisted `stride-claiming-tasks` SKILL.md copy) — now state what a server predating G408's slim-on-index does: it ignores the parameter and serves the 2.4 MB anyway, so on such a server those endpoints are avoided outright. Degrade-SAFE mentions (the complete curl's `?response_view=slim` at its five sites, the `fields=` projection pointers in the dispatcher flowchart and Quick Reference Card) gain a short degrades-safely clause: the parameter is ignored, the full body reads identically, and the cost is tokens only. The dispatcher doc's failed-read disposition now covers a curl that never wrote the capture file, with the identical abandoned/unparseable-record disposition. No curl example changed — prose only. Suite 613/613; all three budgeted SKILL.md files within budget.

### Fixed

- **D272 — the zero-commit-child steal is pinned as a ratchet rather than fixed, and the trade was declined by measurement rather than argument.** A childless completion's window contains only the outer task's mid-window commits, reads residual 1, classifies PURE and is subtracted — and that covered span re-grounds the next childless window's residual back to 1, so k childless children strip k outer commits, terminating in an EMPTY snapshot that collides with `STRIDE_NO_OWN_COMMITS`. The candidate fix (a PRESENT-and-empty D255 owned record on a nonempty window becomes subtract-nothing) was implemented behind a flag and measured: 665 to 652 passed, 13 failed. Four of the failures are the new ratchet assertions flipping — the branch working — and the other **nine are pre-existing pins** (23j, 23n x3, 23o, 23p x2, 23q, 23v). Every fixture whose `after_doing` does not commit records a present-and-empty set on every window, so the branch stops subtracting nested windows at all for hand-committing agents: 23j's outer came back `[fileB.txt, outerA.txt]` and 23p's outermost `[deepC.txt, midB.txt, topA.txt]`. That is D236 reverted for the whole fallback world, not merely W2066 re-opened for 23n — and since 23n and 23v are the same shape to every signal attribution has, differing only in who authored the one commit in the window, no branch keyed on that record can separate them. New test 23v2 pins k=2, the k=3 empty-snapshot terminal shape, the two shapes that do NOT ratchet (an empty window has nothing to steal; a real-commit child's owned record breaks the chain), and two shapes the task's exploratory session surfaced: the victim is the enclosing task at ANY depth (a childless grandchild takes the middle task's commit and leaves the outermost intact), and uncommitted work at completion masks the terminal shape entirely, so the empty snapshot is not this shape's signature. That session also refuted the first draft of the eviction note — inside a live outer's window the 20-window cap is unreachable, so 22 childless windows evicted nothing and all 22 commits were stolen — and the note now records that the eviction which does fire is the open-window cap, whose direction is total loss. Suites 670 sh / 482 ps1.

- **D271 — an outermost task's snapshot keeps its manual commits when `after_doing` commits.** The D255 owned-set narrowing assumed a dropped `base..H0` commit falls back into an enclosing live window — true for a nested task (23u/23w), but an outermost task has no absorber, so the documented over-report became a silent **under-report**: the snapshot carried only what the sweep committed (worst observed: a 22-child outer whose real deliverable was missing). `another_open_window_exists/1` defines open as a `TASK_BASE_REF` with no head partner, excluding self and sentinels, with candidates validated by `rev-parse ^{commit}` plus ancestor-of-HEAD so a stale or garbage cache line can never re-enable narrowing. `finalize_after_doing` narrows only when another open window exists; an outermost task instead unions the owned range with the pre-loop attributed ranges, so neither the manual commits nor the sweep's own commit is lost. The `before_review` self-heal takes the same gate. Tests 23z3-23z7 cover the unit shapes, the empty-owned edge, the 22-children worst case and the stale-line trigger; 23u/23w are unchanged.

- **D256 — mutual sibling coverage can no longer manufacture purity.** Windows now classify smallest-set-first, and a residual is reduced only by D255 owned SHAs (exact, per-commit) and by already-PURE windows whose sets NEST inside it (subset, never mere intersection, never an ambiguous window); ambiguous windows contribute nothing. Two concurrently-open sibling windows therefore both read AMBIGUOUS and the outer keeps its mid-window commit instead of losing it to their mutual overlap — new test 23s2, red-checked against the pre-fix hook. The loss-vs-noise trade is decided for never losing an author's commit and pinned: 23s's and 23x's outer assertions move to the over-report expectation, because those fixtures are topologically identical to the sibling repro and no topology-only rule can keep those outers clean without re-opening the steal. Every failure path in the fixpoint degrades toward ambiguity (over-report), never toward a subtracted span.

- **D268 — per-window, open-window-aware eviction replaces the per-family `tail -n 20` caps.** A still-open task's anchor no longer falls to the cap: open windows (a base with no head) are pinned, newest 20, which is the abandoned-claim aging bound and degrades to the loud refusal. Closed windows inside a live outer's window are all kept so its attribution never absorbs a nested task's commits; older closed windows cap at 20. Eviction is per window across all three record families — the family-desync answer — so head and owned records live and die with their base, and the record writers now skip an orphan pair on a refused completion. Tests 23e2 (21 nested cycles, red-checked against the pre-fix hook) and 23e3 (a refusal writes no orphans).
